SPY vs FRED SP500 cross-check

comparisonfromtoSPY %FRED SP500 %|difference| ppagree (same sign, <= 0.3 pp)window
SPY (adjusted close) vs FRED SP5002026-10-012026-10-081.31.290.011yeslatest FRED window known at the cutoff (not the week)

SPY adjusted close ratio vs the SP500 price index. SPY accrues its constituents' dividends and its raw close drops by a whole quarter of them on its own ex-date, while the index drops as each constituent goes ex-dividend: the adjusted ratio tracks the index (since 2025, SPY ex-date weeks: mean |adjusted - SP500| 0.035 pp vs 0.262 pp for close_raw). FRED publishes SP500 for day d on the next day, so the s_0 value is normally not public at the cutoff; the week statement is cross-checked only when the FRED window known at the cutoff is exactly s_-5 -> s_0 and the stated SPY return agrees.

Method notes (sec_events)

Window (2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00, 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00] (T_p, T_c]; thresholds v2-2026-10-10; event classes: pre-declared map sec-events-map-v2-2026-10-07.

Admissibility uses the SEC acceptance label read as New York time (conservative). Session mapping uses the label read as UTC; TIMESTAMP_AMBIGUOUS marks filings whose session changes under the New York reading. Early closes (13:00 ET) are not modelled: no source in the system.

Filings accepted before T_c under the UTC reading but admissible only after T_c under the conservative reading belong to next week's report. Filings admissible at T_p (previous window) whose session falls in this week (e.g. accepted after the close of s_-5) are listed in a carried-over table with their session and are not counted in this week's classes.

'No filing of class X' is a statement about the pre-declared map: filings the map does not classify (unmapped forms, 8-K items not in the map other than 9.01) are listed and the statement then carries PARTIAL_COVERAGE. Counts over the window (Form 4, Form 144) carry the submissions-feed coverage codes.

A Form 4 counts as read only when all its dated non-derivative transactions are stored, or when its stored XML (read offline, no download) lists none.

Freshness: 10-K / 10-Q family 'quarterly_filing' (120 d); event filings and negative facts 'weekly' (14 d), evaluated at the cutoff; in change claims the baseline view at T_p is evaluated at T_p (its own view instant).

Item 5.02 covers both departures and appointments; the code alone does not say which. An 8-K/A repeats an earlier event (link to the original not parsed). Rule 10b5-1 sales are planned.

No interpretation of filing content is made: classes are item-code lookups only; timing is described with session mapping, no causal link to any price move is asserted.

  • overall: margins, cash flow, EPS, shares outstanding (dilution), guidance, analyst estimates — NOT IMPLEMENTED: not ingested from XBRL company facts (only revenue, debt and equity are stored); analyst estimates have no free point-in-time source

FRED (rank 1). T_p = 2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00, T_c = 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00. Values as known at T are the FRED vintage in effect at T (publication instant = end of the vintage date, New York, conservative; shown as 05:00 UTC the next day); end-of-day daily series are limited to observations dated up to s_0 (T_c) and s_-5 (T_p). Freshness: items as known at T_c are evaluated at T_c, items as known at T_p at T_p. CPI / PCE rows show YoY % computed within one vintage from the seasonally adjusted index (the BLS headline 12-month change uses the unadjusted index and can differ slightly). Revision noise is the largest |value at T_c - first print| over periods first printed inside the stored vintages. Pre-declared materiality thresholds (thresholds v2-2026-10-10, weekly.thresholds.macro_abs_change; heuristic, not validated: HEURISTIC_THRESHOLD): DGS10 0.1, DGS2 0.1, T10Y2Y 0.1, DFF 0.1, VIXCLS 2, BAMLH0A0HYM2 0.1, BAA10Y 0.1, CPIAUCSL 0.1, CPILFESL 0.1, PCEPI 0.1; |difference| < threshold - 1e-09 x max(1, threshold) adds BELOW_THRESHOLD (the tolerance absorbs float rounding). No pre-declared threshold (differences are facts, materiality not assessed): DTWEXBGS, NFCI. Full pre-declared list, moved or not.

Daily values are published with a lag of one day or more, so the latest dates of each week are usually not known at its cutoff: compare the n columns. The revision noise of a mean is bounded by the largest revision of a single daily value. No materiality is assessed on these means.

Inflation block: direction of changes beyond revision noise (sign convention macro-direction-v1)

blockseriesnew release in windowchangebeyond revision noisemateriality threshold (macro_abs_change)direction under the conventioncounted

Pre-declared sign convention: CPIAUCSL +, CPILFESL +, PCEPI + (+ = an increase is 'higher'). A directional reading needs >= 2 indicators with a new release in the window and a change beyond revision noise (and not below its pre-declared materiality threshold when one exists), all pointing the same way; it is always an interpretation (UNCERTAIN by rule).

Method notes (macro)

Window (T_p, T_c] = (2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00, 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00]; week observations 2026-10-02 < d <= 2026-10-09, previous week 2026-09-25 < d <= 2026-10-02. Values as known at T are FRED vintages (publication = end of the vintage date, New York, conservative); FRED gives dates only, no time of day.

Thresholds v2-2026-10-10: macro materiality thresholds applied from weekly.thresholds.macro_abs_change (DGS10 0.1, DGS2 0.1, T10Y2Y 0.1, DFF 0.1, BAA10Y 0.1, BAMLH0A0HYM2 0.1, VIXCLS 2, ICSA 15,000, UNRATE 0.1, CPIAUCSL 0.1, CPILFESL 0.1, PCEPI 0.1; heuristic, not validated): a difference compared with its threshold carries HEURISTIC_THRESHOLD, and BELOW_THRESHOLD when |difference| < threshold - 1e-09 x max(1, threshold) (tolerance for float rounding). Series without a threshold (DTWEXBGS, NFCI, USEPUINDXD, GEPUCURRENT, PAYEMS, CCSA, UMCSENT, RSAFS, JTSJOL, MORTGAGE30US, HOUST, CES0500000003): differences are reported as facts, materiality not assessed (never treated as a threshold of 0).

VIXCLS: 27 stored FRED version(s) available at T_c carry a vintage date earlier than their own observation date (latest: 2026-09-07, published 2026-09-04 (FRED vintage)). An end-of-day value cannot be public before the end of the day it describes, so these timestamps are not credible and the versions are not used.

USEPUINDXD and GEPUCURRENT measure economic-policy uncertainty from newspaper coverage; they are not records of geopolitical events. Official documents on sanctions, export controls, trade and presidential actions (Federal Register, rank 1) are in the geopolitics section.

Macro and socio-economic data are economy-wide: no company-level attribution is made; any link with a company or a price move is timing only ("coincided with").

Directional block readings use sign convention macro-direction-v1 and are always interpretations (UNCERTAIN by rule).

Method notes (agencies)

T_p = 2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00, T_c = 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00. Data from `python main.py collect`; nothing is downloaded by this section. Economy-wide data: no company-level attribution; timing only.

  • overall: consensus forecasts and surprises for macro and socio-economic releases — no source of consensus forecasts is available to this system (no licensed survey data; FRED does not publish consensus)
  • overall: official release times of day for the values in these tables — the tables date FRED values by their vintage date with the conservative end-of-day New York convention; release times of day are given for BLS releases in the catalysts section (BLS release calendar); BEA and other agencies' release times are not collected
  • overall: BLS series read directly from the agency — no value known at the cutoff for 9 BLS series (BLS:CUSR0000SA0, BLS:CUUR0000SA0, BLS:CUSR0000SA0L1E, BLS:CUUR0000SA0L1E, BLS:CUSR0000SAH1, BLS:CUSR0000SA0E, ...); BLS values carry no publication time and count from their retrieval, so only a collect run before the cutoff makes them admissible; `python main.py collect` stores these values; run it daily
  • overall: EIA series read directly from the agency — no value known at the cutoff for 12 EIA series (EIA:RWTC, EIA:RBRTE, EIA:RNGWHHD, EIA:WCESTUS1, EIA:WCSSTUS1, EIA:WGTSTUS1, ...); EIA values carry no publication time and count from their retrieval, so only a collect run before the cutoff makes them admissible; `python main.py collect` stores these values; run it daily

USEPUINDXD and GEPUCURRENT count newspaper articles about economic-policy uncertainty; they do not identify any geopolitical event and are heavily revised (USEPUINDXD: weekly means only, never a single day). Geopolitical event sources (Federal Register documents, GPR index, GDELT event counts) are reported by the geopolitics section; news stories are handled by the news section (aggregator, UNCERTAIN at most). No pre-declared macro materiality threshold for these series in thresholds v2-2026-10-10: differences are facts, materiality not assessed (never "material changes").

Method notes (geopolitics)

Window (2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00, 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00]. Data from `python main.py collect`; nothing is downloaded by this section. Timing only: no geopolitical event is linked to any price move.

Method notes (news)

Official primary documents (Federal Register: OFAC, BIS, USTR, presidential documents) and news-based indices (GPR, GDELT) are reported by the geopolitics section; the geopolitical statements of this section rest on a news aggregator only (UNCERTAIN at most).

CRWD news: 3 further story cluster(s) are listed in the cluster table only (pre-declared cap of 20 conclusions per company).

NET news: 8 further story cluster(s) are listed in the cluster table only (pre-declared cap of 20 conclusions per company).

Window (2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00, 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00]; topics geo-topics-v1-2026-10-07; thresholds v2-2026-10-10; dedup on canonical URL and normalized title; clusters: token-set Jaccard >= 0.6; global story conclusions for clusters with >= 2 distinct outlets (at most 10); company story conclusions for every cluster (at most 20 per company; most outlets first, then earliest); every cluster is in the tables.

NewsAPI requests made by this section: 0; stored identical requests reused: 5. Company queries are name searches, not an exposure map: company-specific geopolitical exposure is not assessed.

  • overall: conflict event datasets curated by researchers (e.g. ACLED) — not ingested (no account or licence configured)
  • overall: geopolitical topic 'export controls' (news) — no relevant article published in the window among the 100 NewsAPI results returned of 4442 (coverage 2.3%): absence of reporting is not established and nothing is concluded
  • overall: geopolitical topic 'armed conflict' (news) — no relevant article published in the window among the 100 NewsAPI results returned of 4442 (coverage 2.3%): absence of reporting is not established and nothing is concluded
  • overall: geopolitical topic 'government shutdown' (news) — no relevant article published in the window among the 100 NewsAPI results returned of 4442 (coverage 2.3%): absence of reporting is not established and nothing is concluded

FRED (rank 1). Window (T_p, T_c] = (2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00, 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00]. A release date is the FRED vintage date; the reference period is not the release date. Changes: absolute for levels, percent for RSAFS, HOUST and CES0500000003; PAYEMS change = monthly payroll change. Prior period: previous month; weekly series: the latest observation dated 4 to 10 days earlier (holiday weeks move a release by a day). "Beyond revision noise" only when |change| > both the largest revision of that change and the largest revision of the level (percent-change series: in % of the first print) between first print and the T_c vintage over the stored vintages. Pre-declared materiality thresholds (thresholds v2-2026-10-10, weekly.thresholds.macro_abs_change; heuristic, not validated: HEURISTIC_THRESHOLD): UNRATE 0.1, ICSA 15,000; |difference| < threshold - 1e-09 x max(1, threshold) adds BELOW_THRESHOLD (the tolerance absorbs float rounding). No pre-declared threshold (differences are facts, materiality not assessed): PAYEMS, CCSA, UMCSENT, RSAFS, JTSJOL, MORTGAGE30US, HOUST, CES0500000003. Official sampling errors are not available.

Pre-declared sign convention: UNRATE -, PAYEMS +, ICSA -, CCSA -, JTSJOL + (+ = an increase is 'firmer'). A directional reading needs >= 2 indicators with a new release in the window and a change beyond revision noise (and not below its pre-declared materiality threshold when one exists), all pointing the same way; it is always an interpretation (UNCERTAIN by rule).

Method notes (sector)

Week 2026-10-02 close -> 2026-10-09 close; cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; thresholds v2-2026-10-10; rule sector-wide-v1-2026-10-07: flag set only when sign(ETF r_W) = sign(peer median r_W) (both non-zero), |ETF r_W| > 1 x the sd of its previous non-overlapping 5-session returns (up to 156 weeks, minimum 52), and at least 2/3 of the peers with a return share that sign (compared on counts) (k from config thresholds v2-2026-10-10; peer share from config thresholds v2-2026-10-10).

Peer lists and sector ETFs were pre-declared in config weekly (chosen in 2026): survivorship and selection bias (delisted or since-reclassified names are absent). ETF holdings are not available to this system: whether the company (or a peer) is held by its sector ETF, and with what weight, is unknown, so mechanical co-movement cannot be excluded. Prices: single rank-2 source (yfinance): UNCERTAIN by rule. Timing only: a sector move that coincided with the company move is not stated to explain it; no causal link is established.

  • CRWD: SEC SIC code at the cutoff — the submissions feed was retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:31:34+00:00, after the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00 and shows only the current SIC (no SIC history; the time that value became public is unknown); no point-in-time SIC (filing header ASSIGNED-SIC) is parsed by this system; the current code is shown in the table for reference only
  • NET: SEC SIC code at the cutoff — the submissions feed was retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:31:43+00:00, after the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00 and shows only the current SIC (no SIC history; the time that value became public is unknown); no point-in-time SIC (filing header ASSIGNED-SIC) is parsed by this system; the current code is shown in the table for reference only
  • RKLB: SEC SIC code at the cutoff — the submissions feed was retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:31:48+00:00, after the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00 and shows only the current SIC (no SIC history; the time that value became public is unknown); no point-in-time SIC (filing header ASSIGNED-SIC) is parsed by this system; the current code is shown in the table for reference only
  • MP: SEC SIC code at the cutoff — the submissions feed was retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:31:51+00:00, after the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00 and shows only the current SIC (no SIC history; the time that value became public is unknown); no point-in-time SIC (filing header ASSIGNED-SIC) is parsed by this system; the current code is shown in the table for reference only
  • overall: sector ETF holdings — ETF holdings are not available to this system: whether the company (or a peer) is held by its sector ETF, and with what weight, is unknown, so mechanical co-movement cannot be excluded
  • overall: peer SIC codes and peer SEC events in the window — not retrieved by this section: it reads the SEC submissions feed of the report companies only (SEC request budget); peer filings are not examined

Values as known at T_p and at T_c (FRED vintages). Rule (documented, not validated): increased = change > 1 sample standard deviation of the Friday-to-Friday changes (last observation of each week) of the series as known at T_p, at least 26 changes, multiplied by sqrt(n) when the two observations compared are n weeks apart (n = round(calendar days / 7), at least 1: a weekly series whose T_p observation is two weeks older is compared with sqrt(2) sd). Higher = more stress for all four series. FIRST_PRINT_WITHIN_REVISION_NOISE when the T_p observation was revised by at least the size of the change. Thresholds v2-2026-10-10.

FINRA short interest (rank 1): twice-monthly settlement dates, disseminated about 7 business days later (conservative publication date). Option snapshots (yfinance, rank 2, unofficial) have no publication time: only snapshots retrieved before the cutoff are used; implied volatility changes have no pre-declared rule. A large change in short interest is not a forecast and not a risk score.

Method notes (risks)

T_p = 2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00, T_c = 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; thresholds v2-2026-10-10. Optional threshold keys honoured if added to config: debt_to_equity_change_abs, debt_change_rel, insider_sale_value_ratio, insider_sale_min_usd, discretionary_sellers_change_abs. Timing only: no causal link and no prediction is asserted.

  • overall: risk_score — NOT IMPLEMENTED: no risk model exists (risk engine is phase P3.1); no aggregate risk score is computed
  • CRWD: option implied volatility (yfinance snapshot) — no option snapshot retrieved at or before the cutoff (snapshots carry no publication time, so only one retrieved before the cutoff is admissible); `python main.py collect` stores these data; run it daily
  • NET: option implied volatility (yfinance snapshot) — no option snapshot retrieved at or before the cutoff (snapshots carry no publication time, so only one retrieved before the cutoff is admissible); `python main.py collect` stores these data; run it daily
  • RKLB: option implied volatility (yfinance snapshot) — no option snapshot retrieved at or before the cutoff (snapshots carry no publication time, so only one retrieved before the cutoff is admissible); `python main.py collect` stores these data; run it daily
  • MP: option implied volatility (yfinance snapshot) — no option snapshot retrieved at or before the cutoff (snapshots carry no publication time, so only one retrieved before the cutoff is admissible); `python main.py collect` stores these data; run it daily

Method notes (catalysts)

Horizon (2026-10-09, 2026-10-24] = the 10 sessions after s_0 (2026-10-09) as calendar dates; cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; thresholds v2-2026-10-10 (no threshold is used by this section). Catalysts are dates only: no direction, magnitude or price impact is forecast, and a scheduled release is not an expectation of its content. Peers' catalysts are not covered.

  • overall: exchange session calendar for the 10 sessions after s_0 — future sessions are not observed (the session calendar is the SPY daily bars up to s_0) and no exchange-calendar library is configured: holidays and early closes in the horizon are unknown, so catalysts are given as calendar dates, never as session numbers
  • overall: Consumer Price Index (FRED release 10): schedule as known at the cutoff — no FRED release calendar stored at or before the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; FRED does not timestamp schedule entries (no vintages for release dates), so a calendar retrieved after the cutoff cannot establish what was known at T_c (calendar retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:32:57+00:00, fetch 359, listed in the table for information only and stored for the next report)
  • overall: Employment Situation (FRED release 50): schedule as known at the cutoff — no FRED release calendar stored at or before the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; FRED does not timestamp schedule entries (no vintages for release dates), so a calendar retrieved after the cutoff cannot establish what was known at T_c (calendar retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:32:57+00:00, fetch 360, listed in the table for information only and stored for the next report)
  • overall: Personal Income and Outlays (FRED release 54): schedule as known at the cutoff — no FRED release calendar stored at or before the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; FRED does not timestamp schedule entries (no vintages for release dates), so a calendar retrieved after the cutoff cannot establish what was known at T_c (calendar retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:32:58+00:00, fetch 361, listed in the table for information only and stored for the next report)
  • overall: Gross Domestic Product (FRED release 53): schedule as known at the cutoff — no FRED release calendar stored at or before the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; FRED does not timestamp schedule entries (no vintages for release dates), so a calendar retrieved after the cutoff cannot establish what was known at T_c (calendar retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:32:59+00:00, fetch 362, listed in the table for information only and stored for the next report)
  • overall: Advance Monthly Sales for Retail and Food Services (FRED release 9): schedule as known at the cutoff — no FRED release calendar stored at or before the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; FRED does not timestamp schedule entries (no vintages for release dates), so a calendar retrieved after the cutoff cannot establish what was known at T_c (calendar retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:32:59+00:00, fetch 363, listed in the table for information only and stored for the next report)
  • overall: Unemployment Insurance Weekly Claims Report (FRED release 180): schedule as known at the cutoff — no FRED release calendar stored at or before the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; FRED does not timestamp schedule entries (no vintages for release dates), so a calendar retrieved after the cutoff cannot establish what was known at T_c (calendar retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:33:00+00:00, fetch 364, listed in the table for information only and stored for the next report)
  • overall: Summary of Economic Projections (FOMC meetings with projections only) (FRED release 326): schedule as known at the cutoff — no FRED release calendar stored at or before the cutoff 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00; FRED does not timestamp schedule entries (no vintages for release dates), so a calendar retrieved after the cutoff cannot establish what was known at T_c (calendar retrieved at 2026-10-11T21:33:01+00:00, fetch 365, listed in the table for information only and stored for the next report)
  • overall: changes of the macro calendar since T_p (added / moved / removed) — no FRED release calendar stored at or before the previous cutoff 2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00: the calendar as known at T_p cannot be reconstructed
  • overall: BLS release calendar for 2026-10 (dates and times) as known at the cutoff — no BLS monthly calendar page of that month retrieved at or before the cutoff is stored (BLS pages carry no version date relied on, so a page retrieved after the cutoff is listed for information only); `python main.py collect` stores these pages; run it daily so that a report has pages from before its cutoff
  • CRWD: next earnings date (as known at the cutoff) — the only source answering (yfinance Ticker.calendar, rank 2) was retrieved after the cutoff and gives no publication time, so it cannot establish what was known at T_c (listed in the table for information); no official earnings calendar is configured (Nasdaq's internal API is deliberately not used); issuer-announced dates in 8-K exhibits are not parsed (NOT IMPLEMENTED); `python main.py collect` stores a daily yfinance calendar snapshot so that a later report can cite one retrieved before its cutoff
  • NET: next earnings date (as known at the cutoff) — the only source answering (yfinance Ticker.calendar, rank 2) was retrieved after the cutoff and gives no publication time, so it cannot establish what was known at T_c (listed in the table for information); no official earnings calendar is configured (Nasdaq's internal API is deliberately not used); issuer-announced dates in 8-K exhibits are not parsed (NOT IMPLEMENTED); `python main.py collect` stores a daily yfinance calendar snapshot so that a later report can cite one retrieved before its cutoff
  • RKLB: next earnings date (as known at the cutoff) — the only source answering (yfinance Ticker.calendar, rank 2) was retrieved after the cutoff and gives no publication time, so it cannot establish what was known at T_c (listed in the table for information); no official earnings calendar is configured (Nasdaq's internal API is deliberately not used); issuer-announced dates in 8-K exhibits are not parsed (NOT IMPLEMENTED); `python main.py collect` stores a daily yfinance calendar snapshot so that a later report can cite one retrieved before its cutoff
  • MP: next earnings date (as known at the cutoff) — the only source answering (yfinance Ticker.calendar, rank 2) was retrieved after the cutoff and gives no publication time, so it cannot establish what was known at T_c (listed in the table for information); no official earnings calendar is configured (Nasdaq's internal API is deliberately not used); issuer-announced dates in 8-K exhibits are not parsed (NOT IMPLEMENTED); `python main.py collect` stores a daily yfinance calendar snapshot so that a later report can cite one retrieved before its cutoff
  • overall: pending corporate actions (shareholder votes, merger closings, offerings) — dates of shareholder votes, merger closings and other pending corporate actions sit in the text of S-4 / 425 / DEFM14A / DEF 14A documents, which are not parsed (NOT IMPLEMENTED)

Method notes (quant)

This signal has NOT demonstrated statistically significant predictive power out-of-sample. The walk-forward (2026-10-07T09:15:12+00:00, 40 tickers) evaluated the signal WITHOUT news (technical, fundamentals, insider): 5d: IC 0.0115, t=0.79, p=0.4295; 20d: IC 0.0198, t=0.8, p=0.4237; required p < 0.0250 after Bonferroni correction over 2 horizons. The live signal includes a news component (weight 0.20) that has never been validated (no news history). Separately, research stage 1 (148 stocks, 2026-10-07T12:15:45+00:00): 0 of 205 pre-registered factor and model tests significant after Benjamini-Hochberg, 0 after Bonferroni. Quantitative signal derived from heuristic scores. It is not a trade recommendation and not a probability.

T_c 2026-10-10T05:00:00+00:00 (closes up to 2026-10-09); T_p 2026-10-03T05:00:00+00:00 (closes up to 2026-10-02); thresholds v2-2026-10-10. News enters only at T_c and only from a daily run computed at or before the cutoff; the technical score uses the last 150 adjusted closes (the walk-forward convention; the daily run uses a 180-calendar-day window, so its number can differ slightly). Scores are never converted into probabilities.

Q11. How reliable is that quantitative signal?

Walk-forward validation of the ex-news combined score (cross-sectional IC)

runcomputed_athorizoncross-sectional ICttwo-sided prequired p (0.05 / k)p < required pcriterion met (IC > 0 and p < required p)approx. 95% interval (IC +/- 1.96 IC/t)folds with positive IC (pooled)note
at T_c2026-10-07T09:15:12+00:005d0.01150.790.42950.025nono[-0.0170, +0.0400]4/10fitted model out-of-sample IC -0.00018; stale at that instant: False
at T_c2026-10-07T09:15:12+00:0020d0.01980.80.42370.025nono[-0.0287, +0.0683]5/10fitted model out-of-sample IC -0.0045; stale at that instant: False

Validation = cross-sectional rank IC of the combined score WITHOUT news over the validation universe (not per company). p is the production two-sided p rounded to 4 decimals and compared strictly with 0.05 / k, as in validation_status (no tolerance: the criterion is pre-declared). A significant NEGATIVE IC does not meet the criterion and is flagged as adverse. DEMONSTRATED also requires a run that is not stale at the cutoff. The interval is a normal approximation from the reported t; "not significant" does not mean "zero". A run computed after the cutoff is not admissible at T_c.

Q12. What does the model NOT know?

  • technical component: daily closes only (no intraday, high / low, order flow)
  • fundamental component: TTM revenue, revenue growth, debt / equity only (no margins, cash flow, EPS, dilution, guidance, analyst estimates or revisions)
  • insider component: open-market Form 4 purchases / sales over 90 days only (no Form 4/A, no derivative transactions)
  • news component: naive word-count lexicon on titles and descriptions, aggregator source, never validated, no history
  • not used by the signal at all: macro, geopolitics, socio-economic data, sector, options / implied volatility, short interest, earnings dates, analyst revisions, events
  • the combined weights (0.25 / 0.40 / 0.20 / 0.15) and thresholds (65 / 40) are heuristic and were never fitted or validated

Missing or degraded this week:

  • overall — margins, cash flow, EPS, shares outstanding (dilution), guidance, analyst estimates: NOT IMPLEMENTED: not ingested from XBRL company facts (only revenue, debt and equity are stored); analyst estimates have no free point-in-time source
  • overall — content of filings beyond form and item codes (exhibit text), earnings-call transcripts, analyst rating changes: NOT IMPLEMENTED: exhibits and transcripts are not parsed; analyst actions have no free point-in-time source
  • overall — consensus forecasts and surprises for macro and socio-economic releases: no source of consensus forecasts is available to this system (no licensed survey data; FRED does not publish consensus)
  • overall — official release times of day for the values in these tables: the tables date FRED values by their vintage date with the conservative end-of-day New York convention; release times of day are given for BLS releases in the catalysts section (BLS release calendar); BEA and other agencies' release times are not collected
  • overall — current University of Michigan consumer sentiment preliminary reading and the source's own publication dates: FRED carries one UMCSENT reading per month, first dated late in that month or early in the next in the stored vintages (not the mid-month preliminary reading), and its vintage date is not the source's publication date; no other source of the survey is configured
  • overall — official sampling-error bands for labour and consumer statistics: NOT IMPLEMENTED: the BLS and BEA technical notes that state sampling errors are not collected or parsed
